a:T6f0,The central idea is Multi-Path Discrete Consistency (MPDC). In continuous space, consistency training works because any noisy state lies on a unique probability-flow trajectory toward the data manifold. In discrete space, there is no such deterministic path, so pointwise trajectory consistency is ill-defined.

The paper replaces that missing object with a stochastic one: the exact posterior bridge q(x_s | x_t, x_0) between two noise levels s < t. For broad corruption families, including masked and uniform diffusion, this bridge is available in closed form. Given a noisier state x_t and the clean sample x_0, one can sample a cleaner intermediate state x_s exactly from the bridge. This yields a family of valid stochastic paths from x_t toward x_0, ranging from direct long jumps to gradual refinements.

CDLM trains a time-conditional predictor f_theta(x_t, t) to be consistent with f_theta(x_s, s) under this bridge coupling. Intuitively, predicting from a noisy state should agree with first hopping to a cleaner state via the true posterior bridge and then solving the easier denoising problem there. Few-step generation then emerges from path equivalence rather than from post hoc distillation.

This is the main departure from prior work. Standard masked diffusion mostly learns local denoising transitions and therefore degrades quickly when the number of sampling steps is reduced. Distillation-based accelerators such as SDTT or DUO+DCD compress a many-step teacher into a short-step student, often in multiple stages. CDLM is single-stage and teacher-free: it directly trains on both short and long bridges from scratch. The framework is also presented as general beyond masked diffusion, as long as the corruption process admits a tractable posterior bridge.b:T576,The experiments focus on the quality-speed-diversity trade-off under limited sampling budgets. CDLM is compared against strong base DLMs trained from scratch, including MDLM and DUO, as well as accelerated or distilled systems such as SDTT and DUO+DCD. The main empirical claim is that CDLM sets the strongest results among single-stage models across a range of sampling budgets, often matching or surpassing multi-stage distilled methods while preserving better sample diversity.

The clearest quantitative statement in the paper is that a distilled CDLM variant achieves up to 32x speedup over autoregressive baselines. Importantly, the paper distinguishes between the base CDLM trained in a single stage from scratch and a further distilled variant used to push speed even further. That separation matters: the contribution is not merely another distillation recipe, but a training principle that already improves few-step generation before any teacher-student compression.

The most informative results are likely the curves over different numbers of denoising steps, base-to-base comparisons against MDLM and DUO, accelerated-to-accelerated comparisons against SDTT and DUO+DCD, and diversity metrics showing that the gains are not coming from collapse. Ablations around bridge sampling, time-pair selection, and the mix of short versus long paths are the sections most worth checking closely.c:T47d,The key to reading the paper efficiently is to internalize the conceptual shift early: in discrete space there is no PF-ODE, so consistency must be defined over a family of posterior bridges rather than a deterministic trajectory. Once that is clear, the objective and the empirical design become much easier to follow.

Start with the introduction and the method overview. Focus on how MPDC is defined, why q(x_s | x_t, x_0) is tractable for masked and uniform diffusion, and how this lets the model learn long-hop denoising directly. Then go straight to the main figures comparing CDLM with MDLM, DUO, SDTT, and DUO+DCD across different sampling budgets. If you have more time, read the section that unifies standard diffusion, consistency, distillation, and shortcut methods under the bridge-consistency view; that part is especially useful for researchers thinking about non-AR language modeling.

A solid skim should take 30–45 minutes. For deeper study, focus on the posterior-bridge derivation, the exact training objective, and the evidence for why few-step quality does not saturate as quickly as in prior discrete diffusion accelerators.d:T853,The main idea is to introduce Binomial flows. The motivation is precise: Gaussian noise is special in continuous diffusion not merely because it is easy to sample, but because it yields Tweedie’s formula, which connects the denoiser to the score and thereby links training to sampling. The discrete setting lacks this bridge. For data living on a non-negative ordered space, the authors propose Binomial noising as the discrete analogue of Gaussian perturbation.

They first define a family of conditional distributions p_{t|T} that maps a clean sample X_T to an intermediate noisy sample X_t, with the usual three properties: easy perturbation, identity at t=T, and complete forgetting at t=0. On top of this flow they train a denoiser m(t,x)=E[X_T|X_t=x] using a time-weighted Bregman-divergence objective. This mirrors the standard denoiser-training view in continuous diffusion.

The technical contribution then comes in three linked steps:
1. under Binomial noising, the denoiser satisfies a discrete Tweedie formula, allowing recovery of a score-like object from the denoiser;
2. this relation leads to a sampling procedure via the Poisson-Föllmer process, which reaches the target distribution exactly in finite time rather than through a purely heuristic reverse chain;
3. the authors derive a likelihood identity involving the Binomial-flow denoiser, enabling exact likelihood estimation and corresponding training.

This differs from many discrete diffusion models that directly learn p(x_0|x_t), a discrete score, or a jump-rate matrix and then sample using approximate reverse dynamics. Here the claim is stronger: with the right noising family, the denoiser alone can support training, sampling, and likelihood in a unified recipe, much closer in spirit to continuous diffusion.

An important limitation is that the framework relies on non-negative ordinal structure. It does not directly apply to unordered categorical spaces, so there is still a gap to standard text tokens, which are nominal rather than ordinal. Extending the idea to language would require additional structure that this paper does not provide.e:T4a7,The experiments appear to validate three points.
1. On synthetic data, the theoretical chain from denoiser to sampling and likelihood is numerically verified, showing that the proposed construction is internally consistent.
2. On real datasets, the method achieves competitive generative performance; the abstract explicitly mentions competitive FID on imaging datasets.
3. Unlike many discrete diffusion papers that only report sample quality, this one also demonstrates exact likelihood estimation.

The provided excerpt does not include the full experimental tables, dataset names, model sizes, FID values, training budgets, or exact margins over baselines, so precise numbers should not be fabricated. What can be stated safely is that the evaluation spans both synthetic and real-world datasets, with the former supporting the theory and the latter testing practical generation quality. When reading the experiments, the most useful checks are the effect of different Bregman divergences, sensitivity to the Binomial noising parameterization, stability of Poisson-Föllmer sampling in finite time, and whether the likelihood computation is truly exact rather than a noisy approximation.f:T42d,Read it in the order definition, theorem, experiment.
1. Start with the introduction and the opening of Section 2 to understand why the authors regard Binomial noise as the natural Gaussian analogue for discrete ordinal spaces. Without that intuition, the later Tweedie result can look arbitrary.
2. Then read the main theorem on the discrete Tweedie formula and the Poisson-Föllmer process. This is the core of the paper. The key question is not line-by-line proof checking, but understanding how the denoiser becomes a sampling object, why finite-time exact sampling is possible, and which assumptions that exactness depends on.
3. Finish with the likelihood identity and the experiments. If the goal is only to assess whether the method is worth following, the synthetic validation figures and the main real-data table are enough.

Estimated reading time is 35–50 minutes. It is more useful for readers working on discrete diffusion, image/audio token generation, or non-AR generative modeling than for readers focused on standard autoregressive text pretraining.10:T7f6,The method has two main components. The first is LAAQ, a layer-aware activation quantization scheme. Instead of forcing all saved activations into a uniform 4-bit format, the paper analyzes sensitivity across layer types and pipeline stages, then assigns different bit-widths accordingly. The motivation is straightforward: attention blocks, MLP blocks, normalization paths, and residual routes do not react to quantization noise in the same way. A uniform policy damages the most fragile activations first. LAAQ therefore treats activation quantization as a structure-aware allocation problem: more sensitive activations keep higher precision, more robust ones are pushed lower, and the average storage cost approaches 4 bits per element without the instability of true global 4-bit activation storage.

The second component is QuanGrad for 8-bit gradient storage and communication. Much of the prior gradient compression literature reduces All-Reduce traffic but still keeps local gradient buffers and accumulation in higher precision, so memory savings are limited. QuanGrad is more aggressive: gradients are stored in FP8, used for local accumulation, and communicated through a precision-preserving 8-bit All-Reduce path. The central issue is that naive FP8 accumulation introduces rounding error that slows or destabilizes training. The paper therefore adds scaling and error-control mechanisms to preserve effective accumulation precision while keeping the communication path in the same low-bit regime. Compared with earlier FP8-gradient efforts, the emphasis here is end-to-end stability of accumulation plus system-level gains.

From a systems perspective, AGoQ is additive rather than replacement-oriented. It sits on top of existing distributed training frameworks: activation quantization reduces forward-saved memory, gradient quantization reduces backward buffers and communication cost, and optimizer-state quantization can still be combined with it. That makes the proposal relatively practical for pretraining pipelines.11:T590,The experiments cover LLMs from 8B to 34B on a 64-GPU cluster, with comparisons against Megatron-LM, Megatron with ZeRO, DeepSpeed, and COAT. The headline result is up to 52% memory reduction and up to 1.34× training speedup, while maintaining training loss and accuracy comparable to higher-precision baselines. That matters because the gain is not only “it fits”; throughput also improves.

The experimental story appears to validate three points. First, uniform low-bit activation quantization hurts convergence, while the layer-aware allocation in LAAQ recovers stability, supporting the claim that activation sensitivity is strongly layer-dependent. Second, low-bit gradient communication alone is insufficient because local storage remains a bottleneck; only when storage and communication are both quantized does the full system benefit appear. Third, compared with methods such as COAT that focus on 8-bit activations, AGoQ pushes average activation precision closer to 4-bit while still preserving training behavior, which is the most important empirical claim in the paper.

The abstract does not provide detailed downstream benchmark numbers, so the main things to verify in the full paper are: whether the training loss curves truly overlap with the baseline, whether the gains hold consistently across model scales, and whether the speedup comes mainly from memory relief, communication reduction, or both.12:T4b5,LightKV is a training-free prefill-time compression method. The central idea is to avoid writing all vision tokens into the downstream KV cache as-is; instead, it aggregates and compresses them during prefill under prompt guidance. The method has three steps. (1) Vision tokens are treated as graph nodes, and edges are defined by a feature divergence metric that estimates redundancy. The goal is not naive token dropping, but information aggregation from redundant tokens into more representative ones. (2) To avoid the quadratic cost of pairwise divergence over many vision tokens, the method partitions tokens into spatial sub-windows and performs local compression and message passing inside them. This reduces complexity and matches the locality of visual patches. (3) The key design is prompt-aware guidance. Rather than compressing vision tokens independently, LightKV uses on-the-fly cross-modal attention scores between text tokens and vision tokens to decide which visual content should be preserved. This is a sensible choice because the prompt determines relevance. The method is designed to be plug-and-play across both vision-encoder-based and encoder-free LVLMs, with no parameter updates.13:T402,The method concatenates image patch embeddings and text token embeddings into one sequence. Images are split into non-overlapping patches with a ViT-style convolutional patch embedding. Captions are tokenized with a Qwen3 tokenizer. A single Transformer processes the joint sequence using a prefix-LM attention pattern: image tokens act as the visual prefix, and text tokens are predicted autoregressively under a standard language modeling loss. The model uses modality-specific embeddings, a unified Transformer, LayerNorm, and an LM head. The design differs from CLIP and SigLIP by replacing contrastive alignment with token prediction. It differs from CapPa, AIMv2, and OpenVision2 by removing the auxiliary text decoder and making the ViT-like backbone receive the language modeling training signal directly. The paper also introduces a second-stage native-aspect-ratio adaptation step, which reduces the gap between fixed-ratio pretraining and real multimodal inputs such as documents, screenshots, and OCR-heavy images.14:T5c2,EOSTok is an end-to-end one-dimensional semantic tokenizer. Architecturally, it follows the TiTok-style 1D ViT autoencoder. Image patches are flattened and concatenated with L learnable query tokens, then passed through a causal ViT encoder. The encoder produces hidden patch embeddings and a 1D latent sequence z; only the query-token latents are kept as the image representation. Quantization uses an IBQ codebook with a straight-through estimator, and the decoder reconstructs the image from the quantized 1D tokens zq together with patch mask tokens. The important change is the training objective. In addition to standard tokenizer losses such as L1/L2 reconstruction, perceptual loss, GAN loss, and quantizer regularization, EOSTok jointly trains the tokenizer with an AR generator. The paper further adds Autoregressive Prediction Reconstruction loss. APR decodes the teacher-forced predictions of the AR model back into pixel space and applies a reconstruction loss there, so errors made by the AR model are fed back to the tokenizer through the final image domain. For semantic supervision, the paper avoids directly aligning the 1D latent sequence to 2D vision-foundation-model features, because that would push the sequence back toward raster-ordered patch alignment. Instead, it aligns the encoder's hidden patch embeddings to VFM representations, injecting semantic information while keeping the final latent sequence free from an explicit 2D layout constraint.15:T40c,Read this one carefully. Start with the introduction's distinction among 2D tokenizers, masked AR generation, next-scale prediction, and 1D tokenizers; that is where the modeling mismatch is framed. Then inspect Figure 2 and Method 3.1 to understand the data path through patches, query tokens, the causal ViT encoder, IBQ quantization, and the decoder with mask tokens. Method 3.2 is the key section: focus on the joint objective and APR loss, especially how AR predictions are decoded into pixels and how that gradient reaches the tokenizer. Method 3.3 is also important because it explains why direct alignment between 1D latents and 2D VFM features is the wrong inductive bias, and why hidden-patch alignment is a better compromise. In the experiments, prioritize the ablations on end-to-end training, APR, and VFM alignment. For reuse in multimodal LM pretraining, the open questions to examine are token length, codebook utilization, AR perplexity versus pixel quality, and the memory and stability costs of single-stage training.16:T6d9,The framework has three main components. (1) Stochastic Condition Masking, SCM. During training, the aligned modality set is randomly partitioned into clean conditions and noisy targets. The targets are generated through the diffusion denoising process, while the conditions are provided as observed inputs. This forces the same text-to-video backbone to handle text-only, visual-only, and hybrid conditioning, instead of specializing to a fixed mapping. The key point is that the task definition itself is randomized, so one model can cover Text→X, X→X, and Text&X→X. (2) Decoupled Gated LoRA, DGL. Different modalities have different output distributions. Sharing one adapter across them causes interference, while full fine-tuning risks damaging the pretrained video prior. The paper assigns an independent LoRA to each modality and activates it only when that modality is a generation target. Condition modalities do not trigger their LoRA branches. This keeps adaptation localized to the output side that actually needs distributional adjustment. (3) Cross-Modal Self-Attention, CMSA. The model shares keys and values across modalities while keeping modality-specific queries. Shared K/V encourages all modalities to read from a common spatiotemporal context, improving alignment; separate Q preserves modality-specific retrieval behavior. Compared with fully separate attention, this increases dense cross-modal interaction. Compared with fully shared attention, it retains modality specificity. The framework is instantiated in two domains: UniVid-Intrinsic for RGB plus intrinsic maps, and UniVid-Alpha for blended RGB, alpha, foreground, and background, both built on top of pretrained video diffusion models with limited adaptation data.17:T438,The experimental story has three parts. First, task coverage. The two instantiations together support 15 tasks across three paradigms: Text→X, X→X, and Text&X→X. UniVid-Intrinsic handles text-to-intrinsic generation, inverse rendering, and video relighting; UniVid-Alpha handles text-to-RGBA, video matting, video inpainting, and foreground/background replacement. Second, data efficiency. The paper emphasizes training with fewer than 1k videos while still generalizing to in-the-wild scenarios, which suggests the pretrained video diffusion prior is largely preserved. Third, compared with specialized task-specific models, the unified model remains competitive across tasks while avoiding the inconsistency issues of serial multimodal prediction. The provided full-text excerpt does not include enough tables to reproduce exact benchmark numbers, but the paper clearly attributes the gains to module-level effects: SCM enables omni-directional generation, DGL reduces parameter interference across modalities, and CMSA improves cross-modal consistency in joint synthesis.18:T692,The method centers on the SciSense framework. Drawing from sensemaking theory, the authors decompose research ideation into eight ordered cognitive stages and use citation neighborhoods as conditioning context, producing full research trajectories rather than short proposals. The dataset, SciSense-Traj, has two modes. In Target mode, given a target paper and its cited works, the model reconstructs a plausible ideation trajectory that could have led from those citations to the known paper. In Infer mode, given the same citation context, the model proposes novel directions without anchoring to a known outcome. These trajectories are then distilled into SciSense-LM models ranging from 3B to 70B.

The most interesting part is not the eight-stage framework by itself, but the supervision design. Intuitively, open-ended Infer supervision might seem more creative. The paper finds the opposite: outcome-anchored Target supervision produces trajectories that are both higher quality and more diverse. The proposed mechanism is that Target trajectories spend more effort on constructive stages such as Schema and Elaboration, which organize prior work into a coherent explanatory structure, whereas Infer trajectories overemphasize early Hypothesis and Questioning stages, leading to broader but less grounded plans. In that sense, the constraint is not merely narrowing the search space; it provides a stabilizing structural prior for a difficult planning problem.

From a training perspective, this is best understood as trajectory distillation: generate structured research plans with a stronger process, then transfer that planning behavior into smaller or more standard LLMs.19:T50b,The main empirical result is consistent and simple: Target supervision beats Infer on both diversity and quality across model families and scales, without the usual quality–diversity tradeoff. The paper reports a 2.0% gain in aggregate plan quality for Target over Infer, while also showing stronger diversity under multiple automatic metrics. It also includes stage-level analysis showing that Target plans emphasize Schema and Elaboration, whereas Infer plans front-load Hypothesis and Questioning.

The downstream transfer result is more important than the planning metrics alone. When these trajectories are used to condition coding agents, Target trajectories improve the quality of generated scientific artifacts, especially scientific grounding, and also help executability and downstream utility. That suggests the planning text is not decorative; it changes downstream agent behavior in measurable ways.

In terms of scale, the dataset is around 100K trajectories and the distilled models span 3B to 70B. When reading the full experiments, the main things to verify are whether the diversity metrics capture semantic diversity rather than surface variation, whether human and automatic evaluations agree, and whether the downstream gains are robust across tasks and model choices.1a:T663,AdaMeZO keeps the SPSA/MeZO gradient estimator as the base primitive. At each step, it perturbs parameters along a random direction z_t, computes a finite-difference loss projection from forward passes, and reconstructs the update direction using the same pseudorandom seed. The challenge is that Adam requires m_t and v_t, two full-size state vectors.

The key idea is to replace exact persistent moments with truncated, reconstructable approximations. First, AdaMeZO uses truncated first- and second-moment estimates rather than full EMAs over the entire optimization history. Older gradients are discarded, motivated by the fact that EMA already downweights them exponentially and their contribution is small relative to recent noisy zeroth-order estimates.

Second, the method exploits finer-grained PRNG replay. Instead of materializing full random directions globally, it regenerates them block by block. This allows the optimizer to reconstruct local pieces of the recent moment history on demand from stored scalar projections and PRNG seeds, rather than storing full moment tensors. In effect, it trades lightweight recomputation for memory.

This is the main distinction from prior adaptive ZO methods. HiZOO explicitly approximates diagonal Hessian information. Helene is closer to a direct Adam-style ZO optimizer with explicit state. AdaMeZO instead focuses on eliminating optimizer-state residency while preserving most of the benefit of preconditioning. The paper also provides a non-convex convergence result showing that the rate recovers that of preconditioned MeZO up to approximation terms introduced by truncation.1b:T444,The experiments are designed to show both optimization behavior and practical LLM tuning gains. On 2D toy landscapes, the optimization trajectories illustrate the intended effect clearly: AdaMeZO follows more stable paths toward minima, while MeZO is more prone to oscillation or poor convergence under the same step budget.

The main empirical section fine-tunes RoBERTa, OPT, and LLaMA on the same task suite used by MeZO, which makes the comparison clean. The headline result is efficiency rather than just end accuracy: AdaMeZO almost always reaches the same stopping criterion as MeZO with up to 70% fewer forward passes, while also delivering better final task performance. That matters because forward evaluations are the dominant cost in zeroth-order tuning.

The most informative ablations are the ones around truncation length, block-wise reconstruction granularity, and the stability of gains across model families. The paper’s claim is not merely that adaptive scaling helps, but that most of the benefit can be retained without paying the usual memory cost of Adam-like states.1c:T53c,Two-stage design:
1) Task-vector analysis. Define task vectors as Δθ between a post-trained checkpoint and the base model. The paper reports three structural properties:
- Magnitude disparity: SFT task vectors have ~30× larger L2 norms than RLVR across layers, so naive linear merging is dominated by SFT.
- Sign interference: ~44.91% of parameters have opposite signs between the two vectors, explaining persistent gradient conflicts in joint training.
- Module-wise heterogeneity: SFT concentrates “important” updates in LayerNorm (reported ~19.9%), whereas RLVR spreads updates across attention, LayerNorm, and the LM head, suggesting partial complementarity.
2) DoTS (Decoupled Test-time Synthesis). Train SFT and RLVR separately, then synthesize at inference: θ = θ0 + λ_sft Δθ_sft' + λ_rlvr Δθ_rlvr'.
- Selective sparsification: keep only top-k% coordinates by magnitude in each task vector, zero the rest, then rescale to preserve the original norm. This is positioned as the key fix for both magnitude imbalance and sign conflicts, reducing sign interference from 44.91% down to as low as ~7.1%.
- Coefficient search: use Bayesian optimization over two scalars (λ_sft, λ_rlvr) on a small set of unlabeled queries, guided by proxy signals such as consistency and perplexity. No parameter updates; only scalar tuning.1d:T542,The paper’s logic is:
1) Underspecification of RLVR: with binary verifier reward R(x,y)∈{0,1}, the objective is equivalent to maximizing Zθ(x)=∑_{y∈C(x)} πθ(y|x). Any policy with the same Zθ(x) is equally optimal, regardless of whether it is fully collapsed or maximally diverse over correct outputs.
2) Collapse via training dynamics: in GRPO, for each prompt, K rollouts are sampled; all correct samples share the same positive advantage A+ (and all incorrect share A-). This means gradients preferentially amplify whichever correct solutions are sampled more often, which increases their sampling probability, creating a positive feedback loop that suppresses alternative correct solutions.
3) What the optimum should be: they define two criteria—robustness under distribution shift and entropy-regularized optimality—and show both select the same structure: the Uniform-Correct Policy (zero mass on incorrect outputs, uniform distribution over all correct outputs).
They then propose Uniform-Correct Policy Optimization (UCPO), a modification of GRPO that adds a uniformity term over correct solutions, reallocating gradient signal from dominant correct outputs toward underrepresented ones. They prove the Uniform-Correct Policy is the unique optimum of UCPO (Theorem 6.1), addressing the original objective’s indifference.1e:T7ca,The paper first reviews several diversity-oriented SFT objectives, including standard CE, distribution-preserving alternatives, and regularized variants. The central claim is that fixing only one side of the problem is insufficient. If one only emphasizes rare patterns, the model may still forget the pretrained distribution. If one only preserves the base model distribution, head patterns in the supervised data still dominate learning. This motivates TOFU, a Tempered Focal loss.

From the formulation and motivation described in the text, TOFU combines two ideas. The focal component downweights easy, already-high-probability targets so that hard tokens and low-frequency patterns receive stronger gradient signal. This addresses the tendency of instruction tuning to concentrate on a narrow set of dominant responses. The tempered component acts as a conservative update mechanism relative to the pretrained model, reducing excessive drift away from the base distribution and preserving alternative plausible continuations that are not explicitly present in the SFT data. In effect, TOFU tries to control two gradient sources at once: the sharp pull from one-hot supervision and the stabilizing pull from the reference model.

Relative to prior methods:
1. unlike plain CE, it does not treat all token errors equally and explicitly reallocates learning signal toward hard and tail events;
2. unlike plain focal loss, it is not only a difficulty-based reweighting scheme, because it also tries to retain pretrained knowledge;
3. unlike pure KL anchoring or distillation-style regularization, it is not uniformly conservative and still allows substantial updates where the supervised signal is genuinely corrective.

From a training-dynamics perspective, the method can be read as a more selective budget allocation rule: spend less update budget on high-frequency, low-information tokens, spend more on tail tokens, and use a tempered reference to avoid catastrophic distributional drift.1f:T4e3,Based on the provided full-text excerpt, the authors run broad evaluations across model families, datasets, and benchmarks, compare existing diversity-oriented SFT objectives in a unified setup, and separately test focal loss as an SFT objective. The qualitative conclusion is that TOFU improves generative diversity over standard CE while keeping output quality competitive. Focal loss alone already helps with low-frequency pattern coverage, but without the tempered reference term it is not sufficient to reliably preserve pretrained knowledge.

However, the excerpt does not include the full experimental tables, model sizes, training-token counts, benchmark names, or exact numerical gains, so it would be unsafe to invent precise improvements. The robust takeaways are:
1. diversity collapse under standard SFT appears consistently across multiple settings rather than as a model-specific artifact;
2. quality-only evaluation can hide distributional narrowing, so diversity and quality must be measured jointly;
3. combining rare-pattern reweighting with reference preservation works better than either ingredient alone.

To extract exact numbers, one needs the main result tables, ablations, and the precise definition of the diversity metrics.20:T4fe,This is a skim paper rather than a line-by-line theory read. Four parts matter most.
1. Introduction: check whether the decomposition into ignorance and forgetting is argued carefully enough to justify the loss design.
2. Theoretical preliminaries: focus on how the paper interprets CE, GEM, FL, and related objectives in terms of probability-mass movement and gradient behavior. The exact derivations are less important than the training intuition.
3. TOFU definition and ablations: this is the key section. Verify what exactly is tempered: logits, probabilities, or a temperature-adjusted target distribution. Also inspect how the focal exponent and temperature trade off quality against diversity.
4. Experiments: pay close attention to the diversity metrics. Distinct-n, self-BLEU, semantic diversity, and judge-based diversity can tell different stories. Also check whether answer quality is controlled via win rate, helpfulness, or task accuracy.

Estimated reading time is 30–45 minutes. If the main interest is method intuition, read the loss definition, the main tables, and the ablations. If the interest is the pretrain–post-train interface, examine whether the forgetting analysis truly measures retention of pretrained knowledge or merely higher output entropy.21:T448,The contribution is platform design and maintenance, not a one-off dataset. Key elements described in the full text:
- A lifecycle model: frequent benchmark launches/updates, human grading where needed, UI/API stability, and ongoing analyses (illustrated by the year-long timeline in Fig. 1).
- Broadened task coverage beyond olympiad-style final answers to proof-based competitions, research-level final-answer questions, detection/rejection of false research claims, and Lean theorem proving (formally verifiable).
- An explicit stance on evaluation reliability: LLM-judging for proofs is biased (self-preference and other distortions), so human evaluation remains the gold standard for key proof subsets; Lean-based tasks trade subjectivity for formal checkability but expose tool-use and long-horizon rigor gaps.
- Emphasis on diagnostic analysis (failure modes, per-benchmark breakdowns) rather than a single aggregate number.
The overarching trade-off is openness and continuous evolution versus the “closed benchmark” ideal, aiming for freshness, anti-saturation, and interpretability.22:T489,The paper combines a formal-language/logic characterization with empirical validation.
- Starting point: prior work (Li & Cotterell, 2025) links fixed-precision Transformer LMs with global attention to a specific fragment of linear temporal logic (LTL) with a single past operator.
- Main theoretical step: prove that adding local attention corresponds to adding a new temporal operator, enabling recognition of a strictly larger family of regular languages, notably locally testable languages (closely related to n-gram-like local pattern properties).
- Complementarity result: show that using only local attention makes the computation depend on a fixed-length suffix, reducing expressivity to definite languages; hence local is not a drop-in replacement for global.
- Positional encoding check: evaluate sinusoidal (SiPE) and rotary (RoPE) encodings and argue they do not compensate for the absence of local attention.
The conceptual takeaway is a sharper mechanism than generic “regularization”: local attention adds a missing kind of local-testability computation, while global attention supplies long-range capabilities that local-only cannot provide.23:T91d,方法核心是 LOCA：在 SAE 概念空间里做最小局部因果 patching。整体流程可以拆成四步。

第一步，建立可 patch 的对应关系。因为原始拒答请求 x_o 和成功 jailbreak x_j 在长度、模板、措辞上都可能不同，不能直接按 token index 对齐。LOCA 先做 token matching，把 x_j 中的 token 与 x_o 中语义或结构上最接近的位置配对，从而允许在结构不同的 prompt 之间做 activation patching。这个设计解决了传统 patching 依赖同构输入的问题。

第二步，把 residual stream 投到 SAE 概念基上。作者不是直接在原始高维残差向量上搜索 patch，而是借助 sparse autoencoder 学到的稀疏概念方向，把每个 token、每一层的表示分解成一组可解释 feature / concept。这样做有两个目的：一是把搜索空间从“任意向量替换”收缩到“沿概念方向替换”，更容易得到稀疏解释；二是让最后得到的干预具备一定语义可读性。

第三步，用一阶近似做 per-token, per-concept 排序。穷举所有层、所有 token、所有概念的 patch 代价太高，LOCA 先用一阶近似估计某个概念替换对拒答目标的边际影响，再按影响大小排序。这里的目标不是简单看 logits 变化，而是看是否朝“恢复 x_o 的拒答输出”方向移动。这个 ranking 相当于一个启发式因果候选筛选器。

第四步，迭代式最小干预搜索。LOCA 不一次性 patch 大块表示，而是每轮选择当前最有用的 token-concept 对进行 patch，然后重新评估后续候选，直到模型从 jailbreak 成功转为拒答，或达到预算上限。这个逐步搜索比一次性 top-k patch 更接近“最小充分干预集”的定义，也能减少冗余 patch。

作者明确对比了 activation steering 与 activation patching。前者通常是在某层对某个方向做加减，问题是可能产生 off-manifold 表示，而且很难知道该对哪些 token 施加 steering；后者直接把 x_o 中对应 token 的中间激活拷到 x_j，对 token 粒度更自然，也更接近分布内干预。LOCA 的主要创新不在提出全新的解释对象，而在把 token matching、SAE 概念化表示、一阶排序和迭代最小化组合成一个可操作的局部因果解释框架。24:T472,This is not a pretraining paper in the narrow sense, but it is relevant to anyone studying how alignment signals are implemented inside a model. Its contribution is less about improving safety performance and more about improving the granularity of explanation. Three aspects matter. First, it moves from global linear explanations to sparse, sample-specific causal explanations. That is useful for understanding what alignment fine-tuning actually changes internally. Second, it makes a methodological case for activation patching over activation steering in this setting: patching stays closer to in-distribution activations and supports token-level causal localization. That distinction is relevant beyond safety, including studies of post-training effects on internal states. Third, the localization findings suggest a layered division of labor: earlier layers rely more on instruction-token changes, while later layers rely heavily on punctuation and post-instruction template tokens. That is a concrete clue that refusal behavior is entangled with prompt structure and chat formatting, not only with high-level semantic recognition.25:T92c,LOCA combines token alignment, sparse concept representations, first-order ranking, and iterative activation patching to produce minimal local causal explanations.

Step 1 is token matching across structurally different prompts. Standard activation patching works best when source and target sequences are aligned. Here, the original refused prompt x_o and the successful jailbreak x_j can differ in wording, length, and template structure. LOCA first constructs correspondences between tokens in x_j and x_o so that patching can be applied even when the prompts are not isomorphic.

Step 2 is to move from raw residual activations to a sparse concept basis using sparse autoencoders. Instead of searching over arbitrary residual dimensions, LOCA decomposes token representations into SAE features or concept directions. This reduces the search space and makes the resulting interventions more interpretable. The patching is therefore not just token- and layer-specific, but also concept-specific.

Step 3 is candidate ranking via a first-order approximation. Exhaustively testing every layer-token-concept intervention would be too expensive. LOCA estimates the marginal effect of each candidate patch on the refusal objective using a first-order approximation, then ranks candidates by expected usefulness. This serves as an efficient causal screening mechanism.

Step 4 is iterative minimal intervention search. Rather than applying a large batch of patches at once, LOCA greedily applies the most promising token-concept patch, reevaluates the remaining candidates, and repeats until the jailbreak is converted back into a refusal or a budget limit is reached. This makes the method closer to finding a minimal sufficient intervention set rather than a broad correlated region.

A key design choice is the preference for activation patching over activation steering. Steering adds or subtracts a direction from activations, which can move the model off manifold and leaves token selection under-specified. Patching copies activations from the refused reference prompt into the jailbreak prompt at matched positions, which is more naturally token-specific and more plausibly causal. The novelty is not a new interpretability primitive by itself, but the way these components are assembled into a practical framework for local causal explanation.26:T6e5,The main empirical claim is that LOCA can induce refusal on a successful jailbreak with about six interventions on average, whereas competing methods generally fail to induce refusal reliably even with a budget of twenty interventions. That is the central result because the paper is explicitly optimizing for minimal causal explanations rather than broad intervention power.

The ablation study is presented as evidence that the gains come from the algorithmic design rather than from a looser intervention budget. From the available text, the likely ablation axes are token matching, the use of SAE concept space versus raw residual space, first-order ranking, and iterative selection versus simpler one-shot strategies. The paper argues that these components jointly explain why LOCA outperforms prior methods.

The localization analysis is the most interesting qualitative result. In earlier layers, the most causally important edits are concentrated on instruction tokens, meaning the user request itself. In later layers, refusal induction relies almost entirely on punctuation and post-instruction tokens, such as chat-template-related tokens. This suggests that refusal behavior is not represented uniformly across depth: early layers are more tied to content processing, while later layers may use formatting and boundary cues to finalize behavior.

The paper also includes a case study showing how LOCA explains one concrete jailbreak success instance. The model under study is a Llama chat model, and the analysis is performed on residual-stream activations. The provided text does not include reliable details on parameter count, SAE size, dataset size, or full benchmark tables, so those numbers should not be inferred beyond what is explicitly stated.27:T5de,Start with the introduction, but read it for the problem framing rather than for background. The key claim is that global explanations are insufficient and that the right object is a minimal local causal explanation for each jailbreak instance.

Then go straight to the LOCA method section. Three points deserve close attention: why activation patching is preferred over steering, how token matching is defined across mismatched prompts, and how the first-order approximation makes the search tractable. If those pieces are unclear, it is hard to judge whether the experimental gains are meaningful.

Next, inspect the main results table with one question in mind: how many interventions are needed before a successful jailbreak becomes a refusal again? The low-budget regime matters most. If LOCA wins mainly by requiring far fewer interventions, that supports the minimal-explanation claim.

The ablation section is worth careful reading. Check how much performance drops when removing SAE concepts, token matching, or iterative reranking. That is where the real source of improvement becomes visible.

Finally, read the localization analysis and the case study. The former tells you where refusal-relevant causal signals live across layers and token positions; the latter tells you whether the discovered interventions correspond to something a human can actually interpret. For a quick pass, method figures, the main result table, and the localization plots are enough for roughly 20 to 30 minutes.28:T977,方法上最重要的是一个分解框架，而不是某个复杂模型。作者构造了七种干预条件，把从原生 CoT 到完整工具 agent 的路径拆成若干可测组件。

主分解链是：NoTool-CoT → NoTool-FCStyle → Agent-NoopTool → Agent-Full。

NoTool-CoT 是普通链式推理基线。NoTool-FCStyle 只把提示格式改成 function-calling 风格，但不给工具访问，用来测量 style cost，也就是仅仅因为输出格式、角色约束、schema 变化带来的性能损失。Agent-NoopTool 在此基础上加入完整工具调用协议，但工具返回 no-op stub，不提供真实帮助，用来估计 function-calling protocol overhead。Agent-Full 则恢复真实工具执行，测量最终净效果。

在这个链条上，作者定义了三个核心量：Δ_sty，格式成本；Δ_frc，函数调用协议开销；Δ_cmp，真实计算或工具执行带来的收益。这样，CoT 与 Agent 的性能差不再是一个黑箱净值，而能被拆成“先损失多少，再补回多少”。

此外，论文还设计了几个诊断条件。Agent-OracleCalc 用金标准工具输出替代真实工具结果，给出“如果工具执行完全正确，最多能补回多少”的上界；Agent-OracleEvidence 提供干净证据，隔离语义干扰主要伤害的是证据选择还是后续推理整合；Agent-Max1Turn 限制最多一次函数调用，用来判断多轮交互究竟在帮忙还是主要增加协议负担。

数据方面，作者没有只在原 benchmark 上比较，而是专门构造了 Sem-Distractor 版本的 GSM8K 和 HotPotQA。干扰分四类：Thematic Background、Semantic Paraphrase、Parallel Entity Distractor、Hedged Uncertainty。共同特点是“语义上相关、语言上自然、但对正确推理无帮助甚至误导”，比简单随机噪声更接近真实场景。

在机制解释上，作者提出 capability overlap principle：很多看似适合用工具的样本，其实 native CoT 已经能做对，因此工具执行的边际收益只覆盖一小部分样本；但工具协议成本会作用于更大范围样本，于是净效果可能为负。最后，作者提出 G-STEP 作为轻量推理时控制方法，用来缓解这种税负。给出的片段没有完整展开 G-STEP 细节，但从描述看，它应当是在推理时更谨慎地决定是否进入工具调用流程，减少不必要的 protocol overhead。29:T966,The main contribution is a factorized intervention framework rather than a new model architecture. The authors decompose the path from native reasoning to full tool-augmented reasoning into measurable components.

The primary chain is NoTool-CoT -> NoTool-FCStyle -> Agent-NoopTool -> Agent-Full.

NoTool-CoT is the standard chain-of-thought baseline. NoTool-FCStyle changes only the prompt format into a function-calling style without granting tool access, isolating the style cost introduced by schema, role constraints, and output formatting. Agent-NoopTool then adds the full tool-calling protocol but replaces actual tool outputs with a no-op stub, estimating the protocol overhead before any useful external computation happens. Agent-Full restores real tool execution and measures the net gain from actual tool logic.

This yields three central quantities: Delta_sty for formatting cost, Delta_frc for function-calling protocol overhead, and Delta_cmp for the gain from real computation or tool execution. Instead of treating the CoT-versus-agent gap as a single black-box number, the framework asks how much performance is first lost and how much is later recovered.

The paper also introduces diagnostic conditions around Agent-Full. Agent-OracleCalc replaces tool outputs with gold answers to bound losses attributable to imperfect tool execution. Agent-OracleEvidence provides clean evidence to isolate distractor-sensitive evidence-selection failures. Agent-Max1Turn restricts the agent to a single function-calling turn to test whether extra turns help or mostly add overhead.

On the data side, the authors build semantic-distractor versions of GSM8K and HotPotQA rather than evaluating only on clean benchmarks. They define four distractor types: Thematic Background, Semantic Paraphrase, Parallel Entity Distractor, and Hedged Uncertainty. These are designed to be topically relevant and linguistically natural, but logically unhelpful or misleading.

For mechanism, the paper proposes the capability overlap principle: many cases that appear tool-amenable are already solvable by native CoT, so the marginal benefit of tool execution is limited to a relatively small subset, while protocol costs affect a much larger set of examples. The paper then introduces G-STEP as a lightweight inference-time mitigation, apparently aimed at reducing unnecessary entry into the tool-calling protocol.2a:T6ac,The experimental story is clear even though the provided text does not include the full result tables.

First, on GSM8K-Sem-Distractor and HotPotQA-Sem-Distractor, native CoT and Agent-Full are compared under semantic noise. The main observation is that tool-augmented reasoning does not reliably beat native CoT and can underperform it.

Second, the factorized intervention results show that the degradation is not explained solely by bad tool outputs. There is measurable loss from adopting function-calling style prompts, additional loss from the tool-calling protocol itself, and the gain from actual tool execution often fails to compensate for those earlier losses. That empirical decomposition is the core evidence for the tool-use tax.

Third, the oracle conditions help localize the bottleneck. If Agent-OracleCalc still does not recover much, then the issue is not only tool correctness but also when the model calls tools, how it integrates results, and how distractors distort the process. If Agent-OracleEvidence improves substantially, then evidence selection under semantic noise is a major failure point. Agent-Max1Turn tests whether multi-turn interaction is useful or mostly costly; the paper's framing suggests that extra turns often add more protocol burden than benefit.

Fourth, the capability-overlap analysis explains why average gains can be negative: the subset of examples with unique tool benefit is limited, while protocol costs are widespread. Finally, G-STEP is used as a lightweight intervention to reduce the tax without retraining the model. The provided excerpt does not contain reliable model names, absolute accuracies, or exact deltas, so those should not be fabricated.2b:T58b,Read this paper in four passes: phenomenon, decomposition, mechanism, mitigation.

Start with the introduction and Figure 1. Figure 1 is the structural core of the paper. Once the relationship among NoTool-CoT, NoTool-FCStyle, Agent-NoopTool, and Agent-Full is clear, the rest of the experiments become much easier to interpret.

Then read the dataset construction section. The important point is not the benchmark names but why semantic distractors are a stronger stress test than generic noise. The conclusions are about semantically plausible interference, not random corruption.

Next, focus on the factorized intervention results. Pay attention to Delta_sty, Delta_frc, and Delta_cmp rather than only to end-to-end accuracy. The informative question is where performance is lost and whether real tool execution recovers enough to justify the protocol.

The oracle and Max1Turn analyses are worth careful reading because they determine whether the mechanism claim is convincing. If gold tool outputs still do not solve the problem, then the bottleneck lies in the agent protocol and reasoning integration rather than in the tool itself.

Finally, read the G-STEP section. Even if it is lightweight, it tests whether the tool-use tax is operationally actionable rather than just descriptive. In continuous domains, consistency training along the probability-flow ODE is a popular recipe to accelerate diffusion. For discrete diffusion, no analogous sample-space ODE exists, making direct adaptation ill-defined. We argue that the natural discre","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-04-30T19:31:02+00:00","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2605_00161.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是离散 diffusion language model 的核心效率瓶颈。DLM 理论上可以并行生成，摆脱 AR 的逐 token 解码，但实际高质量采样往往需要上百步 refinement，速度优势被大幅抵消。连续扩散里，consistency model 可以沿 probability-flow ODE 学到少步甚至一步生成；但离散空间没有对应的样本级确定性 ODE，直接照搬 consistency training 在定义上就站不住。论文要解决的是：在离散 token 空间里，如何给 consistency 一个严格、可训练、又能真正带来 few-step generation 的形式化基础。","importance":"对预训练研究，这篇工作属于新型语言建模范式，相关性很高。它的重要性不只在于把 DLM 采样步数降下来，而在于给离散 diffusion 的“快速生成”问题提供了一个更干净的理论对象：不是去硬造一个离散版 ODE，也不是依赖 teacher distillation 或连续松弛近似，而是直接利用离散 corruption process 自带的 exact posterior bridge。这个视角有两层价值。第一，它把 masked diffusion、consistency、progressive distillation、shortcut 这几条线放进同一个框架里。第二，它说明离散生成模型的加速未必需要两阶段蒸馏，训练目标本身可以直接鼓励长跳跃、少步恢复。","technical_approach":"方法核心是 Multi-Path Discrete Consistency, MPDC。作者的出发点很明确：连续 consistency 依赖 PF-ODE，因为任意噪声层级之间存在唯一确定路径；离散扩散没有这种路径，所以不能要求模型在一条不存在的轨迹上点对点一致。替代方案是转向 stochastic path，也就是后验桥接分布 q(x_s | x_t, x_0)。\n\n对很多离散 corruption family，尤其 masked diffusion 和 uniform diffusion，这个 posterior bridge 可以解析写出。给定更噪的状态 x_t 和原始样本 x_0，可以直接从 q(x_s | x_t, x_0) 采样一个更干净的中间状态 x_s，其中 s < t。这样，t 到 0 的恢复不再被理解为沿唯一轨迹演化，而是存在一族合法的随机路径：可以一步大跳，也可以多步细化，只要这些路径都对应同一个真实后验桥，它们在期望意义下应当给出一致的去噪结论。\n\nCDLM 的训练目标就是让时间条件预测器 f_theta(x_t, t) 与 f_theta(x_s, s) 在这种 bridge coupling 下保持一致。直观上，模型被训练成满足一种路径无关性：从更噪状态直接预测，应该等价于先按真实桥接分布跳到更干净状态，再完成更容易的去噪任务。这样训练后，few-step generation 不是额外蒸馏出来的近似能力，而是 path-equivalence 的直接结果。\n\n这和已有方法的区别很关键。标准 masked diffusion 只学局部相邻时间步的去噪，长程跳跃能力弱，所以采样步数一少就掉质量。progressive distillation、SDTT、DUO-DCD 这类方法通常需要 teacher 轨迹或多阶段蒸馏，把多步过程压缩成少步。CDLM 则是单阶段、teacher-free，从一开始就在训练目标里显式覆盖长路径和短路径的一致性。论文还强调，这个框架不局限于 masked diffusion，只要 corruption process 的 posterior bridge 可 tractable，就能套用。","experiments":"实验重点是 few-step 采样质量与多样性的平衡。作者将 CDLM 与几类基线比较：从头训练的基础 DLM，如 MDLM、DUO；以及蒸馏型加速模型，如 SDTT、DUO+DCD。结论是，CDLM 作为单阶段模型，在不同采样预算下都取得了很强的结果，常常能追平甚至超过多阶段蒸馏方法，同时保持更好的样本多样性。\n\n摘要里给出的最明确数字是：蒸馏后的 CDLM 变体相对 AR baseline 可达到最高 32× 的速度提升。这里需要注意两点。第一，作者区分了“single-stage from scratch”的 CDLM 与进一步蒸馏的 variant，前者已经能和现有 distilled discrete diffusion 方法竞争，后者则把速度再往前推。第二，论文强调的是 varied sampling budgets 下的表现，而不是只挑某一个 1-step 或 2-step 点做展示，这说明方法的收益不是偶然对齐某个固定步数。\n\n从全文结构判断，关键实验应包括：不同采样步数下的生成质量曲线；与 MDLM/DUO 的 base-to-base 对比；与 SDTT、DUO+DCD 的 accelerated-to-accelerated 对比；以及多样性指标，证明少步生成不是靠模式坍缩换来的。若正文给出 bridge path 采样策略、时间对采样分布、以及长短路径混合训练的消融，这些部分最值得看，因为它们直接回答性能来自哪里。","reading_guide":"泛读时先抓住论文的理论转向：离散空间里没有 PF-ODE，所以 consistency 的对象必须从 deterministic trajectory 改成 posterior bridge family。只要这一点想清楚，后面的目标函数和实验设计都会顺很多。\n\n建议先看 Introduction 和方法总览，确认 MPDC 的定义、为什么 q(x_s | x_t, x_0) 可解析、以及它如何把 long-hop denoising 纳入训练。然后直接看主实验图，尤其是不同 sampling budget 下与 MDLM、DUO、SDTT、DUO+DCD 的对比。如果时间还有余量，再看 unified view 那一节，作者如何把 standard diffusion、consistency、distillation、shortcut 放进同一框架，这部分对做新型 LM 范式的人很有启发。\n\n预计 30–45 分钟可以完成一次有效泛读。若要深挖，重点看 posterior bridge 的推导、训练目标的 unbiasedness/consistency 解释，以及 few-step 质量为何没有像以往方法那样快速饱和。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses the main practical bottleneck of diffusion language models: generation is parallel in principle, but high-quality text still requires hundreds of denoising steps, which erodes the speed advantage over autoregressive decoding. In continuous diffusion, consistency models solve this by enforcing invariance along the probability-flow ODE. In discrete token space, however, there is no analogous deterministic sample-space trajectory. The paper asks how to define a principled consistency objective for discrete diffusion that actually supports few-step generation.","importance":"This is highly relevant to alternative language-modeling paradigms beyond next-token AR. Its significance is not only empirical acceleration, but a cleaner theoretical object for discrete diffusion: exact posterior bridges induced by the corruption process itself. That matters for two reasons. First, it gives a unified lens on masked diffusion, consistency training, progressive distillation, shortcut models, and recent discrete distillation methods. Second, it suggests that fast discrete generation need not rely on teacher trajectories or multi-stage compression; the training objective itself can teach long-range denoising jumps.","technical_approach":"$a","experiments":"$b","reading_guide":"$c","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"这篇该看方法定义而不是 headline：若 MPDC 真把离散 consistency 立住，DLM 的主要短板会被削弱；先读公式和少步采样表，别急着信生成质量。","en":"Read this for the formulation, not the headline: if MPDC is the right discrete consistency object, it weakens the main practical objection to DLMs; inspect the equations and few-step tables before trusting the samples."},"metadata":{"institutions":[],"authors":["Hasan Amin","Yuan Gao","Yaser Souri","Subhojit Som","Ming Yin","Rajiv Khanna","Xia Song"],"notable_authors":["Rajiv Khanna","Xia Song"],"tags":["diffusion-language-model","discrete-diffusion","consistency-training","parallel-generation","alternative-lm"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00161","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.00161","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2605.00360","title":"Binomial flows: Denoising and flow matching for discrete ordinal data","abstract":"Flow-based generative modeling in continuous spaces exploit Tweedie's formula to express the denoiser (learned in training) as a score function (used in sampling). In this work we close this gap for discrete non-negative ordinal data by introducing Binomial flows. Our framework provides a simple recipe for training a discrete diffusion model which simul","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-01T02:42:06+00:00","category_id":"llm_pretrain","cover_url":"/covers/2605_00360.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","stat.ME"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作解决的是离散有序数据上的一个基础缺口：连续扩散/flow matching 里，denoiser、score、sampling、likelihood 之间有一套很完整的关系，核心工具是 Tweedie 公式；但在离散空间，尤其是非负有序离散数据上，这套关系并不完整，很多方法只能直接学离散 score 或反向转移率，训练和采样都更绕。作者想做的是给离散 ordinal 数据找一个与高斯噪声相对应的 noising family，使得“学 denoiser”这条路线在离散情形下也成立，并且能导出可用的采样和似然计算公式。","importance":"对 LLM 预训练的直接相关性有限，因为论文面向的是一般离散有序数据生成，不是文本语言建模，也没有讨论 tokenizer、离散序列建模或大规模预训练实践。但从新型语言建模范式的角度，它有方法论价值：一是说明离散 diffusion/flow 不一定非要围绕 score 或 rate parameterization 设计，denoiser 也可以成为一等公民；二是给“离散 Tweedie 公式”提供了一个具体可行的构造，这对未来做离散 token 生成、图像 token、音频 codec token、甚至某些 ordinal tokenizer 的统一建模有启发。贡献更偏理论与生成建模基础，不是当前 LLM pretrain pipeline 可直接落地的方案。","technical_approach":"核心做法是引入 Binomial flows。作者的出发点很明确：在连续空间里，高斯噪声之所以特殊，不只是因为它好加噪，而是因为它让 denoiser 与 score 之间存在 Tweedie 关系，从而把训练目标和采样过程接起来。离散空间缺的正是这个桥。对于定义在非负有序空间上的数据，作者提出用 Binomial noising 作为高斯噪声的离散对应物。\n\n具体上，论文先定义一族从干净样本 X_T 到中间时刻 X_t 的条件分布 p_{t|T}，要求满足三个标准性质：容易采样；t=T 时退化为原样本；t=0 时完全忘掉原样本。然后在这个 flow 上训练 denoiser m(t,x)=E[X_T|X_t=x]，训练目标是带时间权重的 Bregman divergence。这个部分和连续 diffusion 中“直接回归 clean target 的条件期望”是平行的。\n\n关键创新在后面三步：\n1. 证明在 Binomial noising 下，denoiser 满足离散版 Tweedie 公式，可以从 denoiser 恢复与当前扰动分布相关的 score-like quantity；\n2. 利用这个关系，把采样过程写成 Poisson-Föllmer process，从纯噪声在有限时间内精确到达目标分布，而不是只给一个启发式反向链；\n3. 推导出一个涉及 denoiser 的似然恒等式，因此模型不仅能生成，还能做 exact likelihood estimation 和相应训练。\n\n这和常见离散 diffusion 的区别很明显。很多离散方法直接学习 p(x_0|x_t)、离散 score、或跳转 rate matrix，采样时依赖近似反向过程。这里作者强调的是：选对 noising family 后，denoiser 本身就足够支撑训练、采样和似然三件事，结构上更接近连续 diffusion 的统一配方。\n\n需要注意的是，这个框架依赖“非负、有序、ordinal”结构。它不直接覆盖无序类别空间，因此离文本 token 这种 nominal discrete space 还有距离。若想迁移到 LM，需要额外的 token ordering 或分解结构，这不是论文当前解决的问题。","experiments":"实验部分验证了三件事：\n1. 在合成数据上，Binomial flow 的理论关系可以被数值验证，说明 denoiser→sampling→likelihood 这条链条是闭合的；\n2. 在真实数据上，方法能得到有竞争力的生成质量，摘要里明确提到 imaging datasets 上取得了 competitive FID；\n3. 论文不仅展示采样，还展示 exact likelihood estimation，这一点和很多只报生成质量的离散 diffusion 工作不同。\n\n从当前提供的正文片段看，实验章节的具体数据表没有展开，因此无法准确给出数据集名称、模型规模、FID 数值、训练步数或与哪些 baseline 的具体差距。能确认的是，作者同时做了 synthetic 和 real-world datasets，两类实验分别对应理论正确性与实际生成性能。阅读时应特别看：不同 Bregman divergence 的影响、Binomial noising 参数化是否敏感、Poisson-Föllmer 采样在有限步数下的稳定性，以及 likelihood 估计是否真的可计算而不是高方差近似。","reading_guide":"建议按“定义—定理—实验”三步读。\n1. 先看 Introduction 和 Section 2 开头，弄清作者为什么认为 Binomial 是离散 ordinal 空间里更自然的 Gaussian analogue。这里如果没看懂，后面的 Tweedie 公式就会显得像技巧堆砌。\n2. 再看离散 Tweedie 公式和 Poisson-Föllmer process 的主定理。这是全文核心。重点不是逐行验算，而是理解：denoiser 如何转成可采样的对象，为什么能在有限时间精确采样，以及这个“精确”依赖哪些假设。\n3. 最后看 likelihood identity 和实验。若只想判断方法是否值得继续跟，实验部分看 synthetic 验证图和真实数据主表即可。\n\n预计阅读时间 35–50 分钟。对做离散 diffusion、图像/音频 token 生成、或非 AR 语言建模的人有方法启发；若只做标准文本 AR 预训练，可以只读引言、主定理和实验结论。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper addresses a structural gap in discrete generative modeling for ordinal data. In continuous diffusion and flow matching, denoising, score estimation, sampling, and likelihood are tied together by a clean mathematical relation centered on Tweedie’s formula. In discrete spaces, especially for non-negative ordered data, that bridge is largely missing, so many methods learn discrete scores or jump rates directly and rely on less unified sampling procedures. The goal here is to identify a noising family for discrete ordinal data that plays the role Gaussian noise plays in the continuous case, so that denoiser-based training can again support sampling and likelihood evaluation in a principled way.","importance":"The paper is only indirectly relevant to LLM pretraining. It is not about text language modeling, tokenizers, or large-scale pretraining practice. Its value is methodological. First, it shows that discrete diffusion and flow models do not have to be built only around score or rate parameterizations; denoisers can be the central object if the noise model is chosen correctly. Second, it provides a concrete construction of a discrete Tweedie-type relation, which could matter for future work on discrete token generation, image tokens, audio codec tokens, or other ordinal latent spaces. The contribution is more foundational for discrete generative modeling than immediately actionable for current LLM Yet, current approaches are ineffective for 4-bit activations and 8-bit gradients, which would easily cause slow convergence or accuracy loss. To address this, we introduce AGoQ, incorporating two new techniques: 1) a layer-aware activation quantization algorithm that allocates appropriate bit-widths for activations of various layers based on their types and pipeline stages to achieve n","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-01T09:39:03+00:00","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2605_00539.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CL","cs.DC"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作处理的是分布式 LLM 训练里两个最难同时压下去的内存项：激活和梯度。已有方法里，activation checkpointing 能省显存，但会带来明显重算开销；8-bit activation quantization 和 FP8 gradient communication 能进一步省内存和带宽，但一旦把精度继续压到接近 4-bit activation、8-bit gradient storage，就很容易出现收敛变慢、训练不稳、最终精度掉点。论文要解决的不是单点压缩，而是在 Megatron/ZeRO 这类真实大规模训练栈里，把低比特激活存储和低比特梯度存储+通信做成可用方案，并且尽量不改训练配方、不牺牲收敛。","importance":"对预训练研究的意义主要在工程可行性，而不是提出新的建模目标。它给出的价值有三层。第一，激活通常是长序列、大 batch 训练时最大的显存项，若能把平均激活存储压到接近 4 bit，就直接扩大了可训练上下文、micro-batch 和并行配置空间。第二，梯度不再只是在通信前临时压缩，而是直接以 8-bit 形式存储并参与本地累积，这比传统“高精度存储+低精度传输”的方案更进一步，能同时减内存和通信。第三，这套方法强调和 ZeRO、PP、TP 兼容，说明作者关注的是训练系统里的真实瓶颈，而不是脱离框架的 isolated kernel 优化。对做大规模预训练的人，启发点在于：低比特训练不是只能从权重和 optimizer state 入手，activation/gradient 的误差预算也可以按层、按阶段、按通信路径精细分配。","technical_approach":"方法分成两部分。第一部分是 LAAQ，layer-aware activation quantization。作者没有对所有激活一刀切地用统一 4-bit，而是先分析不同层、不同算子、不同 pipeline stage 对量化误差的敏感性，再分配不同 bit-width。文中动机很明确：Transformer 里 attention、MLP、LayerNorm、残差路径的数值分布和误差放大方式不同，统一量化会把最脆弱的那部分先打坏。LAAQ 的做法是把激活按层类型和训练流水线位置分类，对更敏感的激活保留更高精度，对相对鲁棒的部分压到更低 bit，从而把平均存储成本做到“近 4-bit”，但避免真正全局 4-bit 带来的训练崩坏。这里的关键不是单纯 mixed precision，而是把 activation quantization 变成结构感知的分配问题。\n\n第二部分是 QuanGrad，用于 8-bit 梯度存储和通信。已有很多 gradient compression 工作只压缩 All-Reduce 流量，但本地梯度缓冲和累积仍然是 FP16/FP32，所以显存并没有实质下降。QuanGrad 更激进：梯度以 FP8 形式存储，用于 local accumulation，同时设计了 precision-preserving 的 8-bit All-Reduce。论文强调的问题是，直接在 FP8 上累积梯度会因为舍入误差导致收敛变慢，因此他们引入缩放和误差控制机制，尽量保留梯度累积的有效精度，并让通信路径也维持同一低比特表示，避免反复 cast 带来的额外代价。和 Microsoft 之前只做 FP8 gradient 的方案相比，这里更强调 accumulation 稳定性和端到端系统收益。\n\n从系统集成角度看，AGoQ 不是替代 ZeRO 或 Megatron，而是叠加在现有并行训练框架上：activation 侧减少前向保存开销，gradient 侧减少反向缓冲和 All-Reduce 负担，optimizer state 还可以继续配合 8-bit optimizer quantization。这样的设计比较务实，因为它不要求改动训练目标或优化器主流程，迁移成本相对可控。","experiments":"实验覆盖 8B 到 34B 的 LLM，在 64-GPU 集群上与 Megatron-LM、Megatron+ZeRO、DeepSpeed、COAT 等系统比较。论文给出的主结果是：在不牺牲训练 loss 或最终精度的前提下，AGoQ 最多减少 52% 内存，占优场景下训练速度提升 1.34×。这两个数字说明收益不是只来自“省显存后勉强跑起来”，而是同时改善了吞吐。\n\n从实验结构看，作者重点验证了几件事。其一，统一低比特 activation quantization 会明显伤害收敛，而 LAAQ 的分层分配能把这种损失压回去，说明误差敏感性确实有明显层间差异。其二，只做低比特 gradient communication 不够，因为显存瓶颈仍在本地存储；而 QuanGrad 把 storage 和 communication 一起做低比特后，才出现更完整的系统收益。其三，和 COAT 这类 8-bit activation 方法相比，AGoQ 把 activation 平均精度进一步压低到接近 4-bit，仍能维持训练稳定，这一点是论文最关键的经验结果。\n\n摘要里没有给出更细的下游 benchmark 数字，但从引言表述看，作者声称训练 loss 和 accuracy 与高精度基线基本对齐。若精读全文，应该重点核对三类图表：训练 loss 曲线是否真的重合、不同模型规模下收益是否稳定、以及在不同并行配置下速度提升是来自显存释放、通信减少，还是两者共同作用。","reading_guide":"泛读时先看引言和方法总览，抓住两个问题：为什么全局 4-bit activation 不可行，为什么单纯 8-bit gradient communication 不够。然后直接看 LAAQ 和 QuanGrad 两节，重点关注作者如何定义“层敏感性”、bit-width 分配规则是什么、FP8 gradient accumulation 的误差是如何被控制的。实验部分建议优先看三类结果：1）memory breakdown；2）training throughput；3）loss/accuracy 对齐情况。若时间有限，30–40 分钟足够建立判断。\n\n更值得留意的深挖点有两个。第一，LAAQ 的规则到底是经验式 heuristic，还是能迁移到不同架构和序列长度的稳定策略；如果强依赖模型族，泛化会受限。第二，QuanGrad 的“precision-preserving”具体依赖哪些缩放和累积技巧，是否会在更大规模、更长训练步数、或更激进的 optimizer 设置下暴露误差积累问题。这决定它更像一套稳健配方，还是一组在特定设置下成立的工程技巧。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"The paper targets two dominant memory terms in distributed LLM training: activations and gradients. Existing options each leave a gap. Activation checkpointing saves memory but adds recomputation overhead. Prior activation quantization and low-precision gradient communication help, but pushing activations toward 4-bit and gradients toward 8-bit storage usually causes slower convergence or accuracy degradation. The actual problem here is not isolated compression, but making low-bit activation storage plus low-bit gradient storage/communication usable inside real Megatron/ZeRO-style training stacks without changing the training recipe or breaking convergence.","importance":"Its contribution is mainly on training systems for pretraining, not on a new modeling objective. The practical impact is threefold. First, activations are often the largest memory component at long context and large micro-batch, so near-4-bit activation storage directly expands the feasible training regime. Second, the paper goes beyond the common pattern of compressing gradients only for communication; it stores gradients in 8-bit form for local accumulation as well, which attacks both memory and bandwidth. Third, the method is designed to work with PP, TP, and ZeRO-style setups, so it addresses bottlenecks that matter in actual large-scale pretraining. The broader lesson is that low-bit training should not be viewed only through weights and optimizer states; activation and gradient error budgets can also be allocated structurally, by layer and by communication path.","technical_approach":"$10","experiments":"$11","reading_guide":"For a skim, start with the introduction and method overview to isolate two questions: why global 4-bit activation storage fails, and why 8-bit gradient communication alone is not enough. Binarization can compress weights to 1 bit, fundamentally lowering compute and bandwidth cost. However, existing methods cannot address activation heavy tails and thus must keep activations in high precision, preventing true end-to-end acceleration. To overcome this limitation, we propose BWLA (Binarized Weights and Low-bit Activations), the first po","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-01T05:42:57+00:00","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2605_00422.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.LG","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"BWLA 处理的是 W1AX 后训练量化的两个主要瓶颈：LLM 权重按通道多为单峰、近似高斯分布，和 ±1 二值码本不匹配；激活存在明显重尾和极端 outlier，低比特量化时误差被少数大值主导。已有二值化 PTQ 多保留高精度激活，推理时仍要反量化权重，难以获得端到端低比特执行收益。","importance":"这项工作更偏 efficient serving，但对预训练研究也有间接参考价值。它把二值权重量化的难点从“只看权重误差”推进到“权重分布形状和激活分布形状的联合可量化性”。这对低比特预训练、QAT 预训练、BitNet 类架构都有启发：如果训练过程能主动形成更适合二值码本的权重分布，并控制激活重尾，后续量化会容易很多。贡献量级主要在 PTQ 方法层面，不是新的基座训练范式。","technical_approach":"BWLA 的核心是用正交变换同时改造权重和激活。对线性层而言，正交矩阵满足 R^{-1}=R^T，因此可以在权重侧和激活侧成对插入变换，在保持前向等价的同时改变数值分布。Orthogonal-Kronecker Transformation, OKT, 通过梯度无关的目标鼓励权重从单峰分布变成更接近对称双峰分布，使其更贴合 {-1,+1} 二值码本。它用 EM 风格的条件最小化求解正交矩阵，并用 Kronecker 分解把一个大正交矩阵拆成两个小正交矩阵，降低 PTQ 阶段的计算和存储开销。同一个变换作用到激活后，还可以削弱激活 outlier 的影响。Proximal SVD Projection, PSP, 在对齐后的坐标系里加入轻量低秩修正，用近端上界和截断 SVD 吸收残余 outlier，减少二值化后的局部误差。和 BiLLM、ARB-LLM 这类主要依赖 Hessian 或显著性保留的 weight-only 二值化不同，BWLA 明确把低比特激活纳入目标；和 BitNet a4.8 这类 QAT 路线不同，它不需要端到端再训练。","experiments":"可见内容给出的关键设定是 1-bit 权重加低比特激活，例如 W1A6；对比对象包括 BiLLM、ARB-LLM 等后训练二值化方法，以及需要 QAT 的 BitNet a4.8。动机实验显示，现有二值化方法在权重量化后若继续把激活压到低比特，精度会明显受损，因此通常保留高精度激活。文中还用 LLaMA2-70B 的 FP16 推理内存超过 120GB 说明部署压力。提供的正文片段没有包含完整结果表、困惑度数值、下游任务分数或逐层消融数字；能确认的是作者声称在多个 LLM 上优于既有 PTQ 方法，并实现纯 PTQ 下的 W1AX 高精度量化。","reading_guide":"建议泛读，约 20–30 分钟。重点看三处：第一，Introduction 中对 W1AX 失败原因的分解，尤其是权重单峰分布和激活重尾的图示；第二，Method 中 OKT 的目标函数、EM 式更新和 Kronecker 参数化，这是方法的主要信息量；第三，PSP 的低秩修正是否真的带来稳定增益，需要结合消融表看。若只关心预训练，可把它当作低比特友好训练目标的参考：训练时是否应显式约束权重双峰性和激活 outlier，而不是事后补救。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"BWLA targets the W1AX post-training quantization setting: 1-bit weights with low-bit activations. The main failure modes are distributional. Per-channel LLM weights are usually unimodal and quasi-Gaussian, which is poorly matched to a binary ±1 codebook. Activations have heavy tails and extreme outliers, so low-bit activation quantization is dominated by a small number of large values. Existing binarization methods often keep activations in high precision, which limits real end-to-end acceleration.","importance":"The work is primarily an inference-efficiency contribution, but it is still informative for pretraining researchers. It frames low-bit model quality as a joint property of weight geometry and activation statistics, not just a local weight reconstruction problem. This matters for low-bit pretraining and BitNet-style model design: a training recipe that naturally produces bimodal weights and controlled activation tails should be easier to quantize than a standard dense model repaired after training. The contribution is methodological within PTQ rather than a new pretraining objective.","technical_approach":"BWLA inserts an orthogonal transformation around linear layers. Because an orthogonal matrix satisfies R^{-1}=R^T, the transformation can be paired across weights and activations while preserving the original forward computation. Orthogonal-Kronecker Transformation, or OKT, optimizes a gradient-free objective that encourages weights to form symmetric bimodal clusters aligned with the {-1,+1} codebook. The orthogonal matrix is solved through an EM-style conditional minimization, and a Kronecker factorization represents the large transform using two smaller orthogonal matrices. This keeps the PTQ overhead small. The same transform also acts on activations and reduces the effect of outliers. Proximal SVD Projection, or PSP, adds a lightweight low-rank correction in the transformed coordinate space, using a proximal upper bound and truncated SVD to absorb remaining outliers. Compared with BiLLM and ARB-LLM, BWLA is not only weight-centric. Compared with BitNet a4.8, it avoids full quantization-aware training.","experiments":"The visible text establishes the main regime as W1AX, for example 1-bit weights with 6-bit activations. The baselines discussed include BiLLM and ARB-LLM for post-training binarization, and BitNet a4.8 as a QAT-based reference for 1-bit weights with 4-bit activations. The motivation study shows that existing binary-weight PTQ methods degrade sharply once activations are also quantized to low precision, which explains why many of them retain full-precision activations. The paper also cites LLaMA2-70B requiring more than 120 GB at FP16 as the deployment pressure point. The provided manuscript excerpt does not include the final numerical tables, perplexity values, task scores, or ablation numbers; the reported claim is that BWLA outperforms prior PTQ methods across multiple LLMs under pure post-training quantization.","reading_guide":"Skim in about 20–30 minutes. Focus first on the distribution analysis in the introduction, especially the figures contrasting unimodal weights and heavy-tailed activations. Then read the OKT formulation, its EM-style update, and the Kronecker parameterization. Finally, check the PSP ablation if the full tables are available, since that determines whether the low-rank correction is essential or mostly a cleanup step. We argue that this request-level abstraction is fundamentally mismatched to compound AI workloads, and propose a shift to program-level scheduling: treating the entire agent workflow (not individual inference calls) as the first-class schedulable unit. Compared with vLLM, SGLang, and Orca, SAGA changes the abstraction from request-centric serving to program-level scheduling. pr","starred":false,"priority":"p3","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-01T17:11:39+00:00","category_id":"efficient","cover_url":"/covers/2605_00789.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CV","cs.AI","cs.LG"],"summary":{"core_problem":"这篇工作解决的是 First, the systems bottleneck in LVLMs is structurally different from that in LLMs: the visual prefix explosion during prefill is often the dominant memory issue. That makes tokenization and token budget design a first-order modeling choice, not just a serving detail. Second, the paper shows that text-guided visual compression works better than vision-only compression, suggesting that cross-modal attention is not only a modeling mechanism but also a useful runtime signal for information selection. That matters for multimodal tokenizers, long-context multimodal modeling, and training-time allocation of visual tokens.","technical_approach":"$12","experiments":"The evaluation covers eight open-source LVLMs and eight benchmarks, including LLaVA-v1.5 7B/13B, LLaVA-NeXT 7B/13B, InternVL2-8B, EVE-7B-v1, EVE-7B-v1-HD, and Qwen2.5-VL. The headline result is that LightKV reduces the KV memory of vision tokens by 50% while largely preserving performance, and in some cases even improving over the vanilla model. Under the same generation token budget, it also reduces FLOPs by about 40%. The most important empirical claim is that prompt-aware compression outperforms prior vision-only baselines, which is the main evidence that cross-modal guidance is doing real work. Another important point is that compression happens during prefill rather than after the cache is already populated, so it directly lowers peak memory. 拼接成一个序列后送入同一个 Transformer。模型包含模态相关 embedding、统一 Transformer、LayerNorm 和 LM head。训练时使用标准自回归语言建模损失，主要监督文本 token 的预测。关键不在于新模块，而在于 attention 结构：图像 token 作为 prefix 提供条件，文本 token 按因果方式预测后续语言。这样 ViT 不再通过 To counteract this, we propose Persistent Visual Memory (PVM), a lightweight learnable module designed to ensure sustained, on-demand visual perception. Integrated as a parallel","starred":false,"priority":"p2","reading_tier":"skim","source":"arxiv","sources":["arxiv"],"published_at":"2026-05-01T17:54:37+00:00","category_id":"multimodal","cover_url":"/covers/2605_00814.png","hf_upvotes":0,"arxiv_categories":["cs.CV","cs.AI"],"summary":{"core_problem":"PVM 解决的是 LVLM 长生成过程中视觉 token 注意力被不断增长的文本历史稀释，导致模型后段生成更依赖语言先验、较难持续读取原始视觉证据的问题。","importance":"这篇论文不属于核心预训练方法，但对 MLLM 架构设计有参考价值。它把长上下文 LVLM 的一个常见现象写成了清晰的机制假设：视觉 token 是一次性注入的固定前缀，文本 token 会随生成不断增长，softmax attention 的分母随上下文变大后，视觉注意力质量会下降。这个分析对设计 native multimodal backbone、视觉 memory、长链推理中的 grounding 机制都有启发。","technical_approach":"方法是在 Transformer block 的 FFN 旁边增加一个轻量并行分支 Persistent Visual Memory。原 FFN 继续承载语言生成和推理状态，PVM 分支通过 gated cross-attention 专门访问原始视觉 embedding。这样视觉信息不再只依赖自注意力里离当前位置很远的前缀 token，而是有一条与生成距离无关的检索通道。论文先把 attention softmax 分母拆成视觉部分和文本历史部分，说明文本历史长度增加会降低视觉 token 的相对概率质量；随后用并行视觉检索分支缓解这一结构性衰减。与直接重新插入视觉 token 的方法相比，PVM 避免频繁扰动主生成序列的隐状态，试图在视觉 grounding 和语言连贯性之间做更稳的分工。","experiments":"实验基于 Qwen3-VL 系列，在 8 个多模态 benchmark 上评估 general understanding 和 scientific reasoning。论文报告 8B 版本加入 PVM 后平均准确率提升 4.8%，4B 版本提升 4.4%。作者还做了长生成场景下的视觉信号衰减分析，显示 PVM 对长度诱发的视觉注意力下降更稳。LogitLens 分析用于检查内部预测动态，结论是 PVM 加速了模型对正确答案的内部收敛，而不只是因为增加了一点参数量。","reading_guide":"泛读即可，预计 20 到 30 分钟。优先看第 3 节的 Visual Signal Dilution 推导，判断假设是否依赖过强的 attention 分布简化；再看 PVM 模块结构图和 gated cross-attention 的插入位置；最后看 4B/8B 的消融表、长生成长度分桶结果和 LogitLens 分析。若关注 MLLM 预训练架构，可以重点思考 PVM 是否应该只在 post-training 阶段插入，还是应在视觉语言预训练阶段一起训练。","authors_note":""},"summary_en":{"core_problem":"PVM addresses visual signal dilution in autoregressive LVLMs: as the generated text history grows, attention to the fixed visual prefix weakens, so later tokens rely more on language priors and less on the original image evidence.","importance":"This is not a core pretraining paper, but it is useful for multimodal model architecture. It gives a concrete mechanism for a common long-generation failure mode in LVLMs. Visual tokens are injected once, while text tokens keep accumulating. The softmax attention denominator grows with the textual context, which can reduce the relative attention mass assigned to visual evidence. This framing is relevant for native multimodal pretraining, persistent memory design, and grounded long-chain reasoning.","technical_approach":"The method adds a lightweight Persistent Visual Memory branch in parallel with the FFN inside Transformer blocks. The original FFN path preserves the main autoregressive reasoning state. The PVM branch uses gated cross-attention to retrieve from the original visual embeddings directly. This creates a distance-agnostic visual retrieval path, rather than relying only on self-attention to far-away visual prefix tokens. The paper first decomposes the attention partition function into visual mass and textual-history mass, then argues that the latter grows with generation length and suppresses visual attention. Compared with methods that repeatedly inject visual tokens into the main sequence, PVM is less invasive: it strengthens access to visual evidence without constantly perturbing the serial language-generation state.","experiments":"The experiments are built on the Qwen3-VL family and cover eight multimodal benchmarks, including general understanding and scientific reasoning. The reported average accuracy gain is 4.8% for the 8B model and 4.4% for the 4B model. The paper also evaluates extended-generation settings and shows improved robustness against length-induced visual decay. A LogitLens analysis suggests that PVM helps internal predictions converge earlier, rather than merely benefiting from a small increase in parameter count.","reading_guide":"A 20- to 30-minute skim is enough. Start with the Visual Signal Dilution derivation and check how strong the assumptions are about attention score distributions. Then read the module design: where the PVM branch is inserted, how gated cross-attention is parameterized, and whether visual embeddings are frozen or updated. Finally inspect the 4B/8B ablations, length-bucket results, and LogitLens plots. For pretraining-oriented readers, the main question is whether this kind of persistent visual retrieval should be added only during post-training, or trained from the vision-language pretraining stage.","authors_note":""},"recommendation":{"zh":"看视觉 attention 随长度衰减的证据；模块本身未必通用，但这个 failure mode 值得纳入 LVLM 评测。","en":"Inspect the evidence for length-driven visual-attention decay; the module may not generalize, but the failure mode should enter LVLM evals."},"metadata":{"institutions":["Shanghai AI Laboratory","Shanghai Jiao Tong University","The Chinese University of Hong Kong","Nanjing University","Tongji University","Wuhan University"],"authors":["Siyuan Huang","Xiaoye Qu","Yafu Li","Tong Zhu","Zefeng He","Muxin Fu","Daizong Liu","Wei-Long Zheng","Yu Cheng"],"notable_authors":["Yu Cheng"],"tags":["LVLM","visual-memory","attention-analysis","long-generation","Qwen3-VL"],"links":{"arxiv":"https://arxiv.org/abs/2605.00814","pdf":"https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.00814","github":null,"project":null,"source_url":null,"article":null}}},{"id":"2605.00371","title":"GaMMA: Towards Joint Global-Temporal Music Understanding in Large Multimodal Models","abstract":"In this paper, we propose GaMMA, a state-of-the-art (SoTA) large multimodal model (LMM) designed to achieve comprehensive musical content understanding.
